Suite 2628 passed / 10 xfailed; freestanding 36 passed (riscv32 + arm64). All ten x-files behave as codex requires — #1/#2/#3/#5/#10 refused in the typechecker, #4 in the required order (iterator deinit BEFORE \"after driven loop\", the owned cell likewise), #6 drops once, #7/#8/#9 compile and run.\n\n**Kept / redone of the stopped agent's work.** E (20ae4f2d, #7 + #9) KEPT as committed: verified the catch rename runs through `_uniq_walk_block` rename-only and its pin covers all seven cells (struct-literal field, `move error`, map literal, closure body, interpolation, `match error`, an inner `let error` shadow left alone); `lends` contextual with six ordinary positions pinned. Its gate had never run — the B gate below covered both. B's working tree (#3 + #8) KEPT after review — `paths_overlap` in `windows.py` is the one definition with `DYNAMIC_INDEX` beside it and `types._paths_overlap` delegates; `window_conflict` resolves the current binding and the table compares `root_id` before projections — plus one cell added: the refining shadow in a DRIVEN body (frame-field shadow). Committed as ddb58e42.\n\n**A — scan-vs-path (#1, #2, #10) — e139823a.** Three rules that read a SHAPE where the question was about a VALUE. #1: `_producer_paths_missing_origin` follows the returning paths (tail, every `return`, each arm of a value branch via `producers.branch_arm_sources`) and refuses the path whose leaf is not a `lends self` construction, by line. #2: `borrowing_struct_name` is the ONE resolver both boundaries ask and walks a distinct alias to its underlying; the value fence asks `_first_borrowing_struct_in` (a composition is refused as the bare value), and the own-methods exemption is for the receiver READ itself — `Alias(self)`, a tuple of `self`, `{ self }` refused at the expression, `let t = self` at the binding, a closure's inferred return checked. #10: `shared_reference_field_hop` is one walk (the hops `_immutable_lvalue_root` takes) asked from `_check_write_target` (3b), `_immutable_receiver_root` (`&var self` call, exclusive window, `take()`) and `_check_reference_expr` (`&var` arg). Sweep found the READ side dead too (`self.source.count()`, `&self.source` ICEd `Owner* != Owner**`): `_get_member_pointer` yields the referent's address for exactly a `borrows struct`'s reference field, by declaration not LLVM shape.\n| shape | base | r1 | now |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| foreign origin in `if` arm / `return` / `match` arm / `catch`, own accessor tail, local handed back | accepted | accepted | refused at the path |\n| every leaf constructs (return + match + nested if) | runs | runs | runs 17 |\n| alias as param / return / field / type arg / static / Item | accepted | accepted | refused |\n| `Alias(self)`, `(self, 1)`, `let t = self`, `{ self }` in own methods | accepted | accepted | refused |\n| `self.source.n = 99`, `+= 1`, `.bump()`, `&var self.source.n`, `.items.push`, `.spare.take()`, `.items[0] = 5` | ICE | ICE | refused, naming the field + rule |\n| `self.source.count()`, `&self.source`, `&self.source.items`, generic, driven | ICE | ICE | runs |\nPins: origin/type/value pins extended (+6/+6/+4 rows), `borrowing_producer_every_path.saw`, `errors/borrowing_field_is_read_only.saw` + `borrowing_field_reads_compose.saw` (K150).\n\n**B — second implementations (#3, #8) — ddb58e42.**\n| shape | base | r1 | now |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `bins[i].iter()` + `bins[0].push` | runs (pushes into the iterated vector) | same | refused |\n| `bins[0].iter()` + `bins[1].push` | 6 | 6 | 6 |\n| shadow `var v = v.copy()` + push, sync AND driven | 2 | refused | 2 |\n| unshadowed `v.push` in the body | runs (K132 UAF) | refused | refused |\nPins: K134 dynamic-index row, K135 disjoint-constant + shadow (sync, driven).\n\n**C — the resource's extent (#4, #5) — 6ae99a3d.** #4: the seed and the loop are one `ScopedBlock`; TWO mechanisms had to agree: `_collect_frame_locals` did not walk a `ScopedBlock` (the seed got no frame field — undefined name once wrapped), and the renamer's scope map is built BEFORE the normalization runs, so `_block_scope_names` answered nothing and no E-FALL/E-BRK/E-CNT/E-RET release existed for the seed — the normalization writes its own `_scope_binders` entry. #5: the head goes through `_check_value_transfer` (entry 20, \"`for` head\") at `_check_for_loop`, ahead of both lowerings.\n| shape | base | r1 | now |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| driven: exhaustion, `break`, `return`, `continue` to OUTER loop, error propagated to caller; owned twin (exhaustion, `break`) | deinit AFTER the code past the loop | same | BEFORE, every route |\n| `for` inside a split `try { } catch { }` whose body fails, driven | late | late | late — DF-242a's standing bound (every frame local of that block); sync twin at the edge |\n| `for n in it`, NoCopy binding, sync / driven | double drop | double drop | refused (move hint) |\n| ExplicitCopy binding bare | copied silently | same | refused |\n| `for n in move it`, sync / driven | once | once | once, before the code after |\n| Copy-tier binding iterated twice, read after; `it.copy()` head | runs | runs | runs |\nPins: K137 rewritten to print INSIDE the walk with the driven routes + owned twin; K140's \"caught and continued\" cell had pinned the late order and now shows the deinit ahead of the in-task print; `errors/for_head_is_a_transfer.saw` + `for_head_transfer_accepts.saw` (K151).\n\n**D — the try landing's error edge (#6) — ac862274.** Replaying every clear was wrong because one statement with interleaved tries and moves has no single right replay set; the statement is SPLIT at each nested propagating `try` in evaluation order before landings are built (`_split_leaf_at_propagating_tries`, after the ANF/try hoists, only when the statement holds both such a `try` and a `move`): a hoisted `let __anfN = try <subject>` owes the clears of the moves in ITS subject only; the residual statement's moves trail the straight-line edge; impure siblings before a lifted `try` are lifted with it (design 147), a pure `move` operand stays in place. EVERY ordering is reachable (drop counts of `kept`: sync / base driven / r1 / now):\n| shape | sync | base | r1 | now |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 `sink(try f(), move r)` (codex f6) | 1 | 1 | 0 leak | 1 |\n| 2 `sink_rev(move r, try f())` — the orphan ordering | 0 LEAK | 1 | 0 | 1 |\n| 3a/3b two tries, move between, first / second fails | 1 / 0 | 1 / 1 | 0 / 0 | 1 / 1 |\n| 4 `(try mk()).use(move r)` receiver | 1 | 1 | 0 | 1 |\n| 5 block form, both orders | 1 / 0 | 1 / 1 | 1 / 1 | 1 / 1 |\n| 6 `return try take(move r)` (SL-339's half) | 1 | 2 double | 1 | 1 |\n| 7 move in the catch; 8 `two(eat(move r), try f())`; 9 two moves × two tries; 10 `sink(g(try f()), move r)`; 11 success path | 1 | 1 | 1 (10: 0) | 1 |\nRow 2's SYNC leak (an abandoned call's already-moved argument is released by nothing) is pre-existing codegen, filed as **SL-348** with its sibling positions; the driven oracle there is the base's count. Pin: `coro_try_landing_relinquishes_by_position.saw` (K152; INDEX recounted 574 rows, 297 here, 277 elsewhere). Final touch (SL-348 cited in the pin/row): 6472c14c.\n\n**E — lexical slips (#7, #9) — 20ae4f2d** (stopped agent's, verified as above).\n\nNot revised: p7 stays at r1 for the lead's re-validation and revise to r2.\n","created":"1790090552","id":"c78"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"Revised patch SL-318.p7 to revision 2","created":"1790090858","id":"c79"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"LEAD RE-VALIDATION of U3 r2 (Sep 22; tip 6472c14c, 11 commits on main, squashed with `git diff main...sl318u3`: 69 files, +7319/-226, `diff --check` clean, applies on main). `patch revise SL-318.p7` = r2.\n\nCodex's ten r1 programs re-run on the tip by the lead (staged as `.build/scratch/lead/x_codex_r1_f<N>.saw`): #1 REFUSED at the foreign-origin path by line (\"every returning path of `bad` must construct `It` with `lends self`… the path ending at line 7 yields the call `.iter()`\"); #2 REFUSED four ways (the `Alias?` return, the `Item`, the `Alias(self)` value, the `saved` composition); #3 REFUSED (`bins[…]` charged conservatively); #4 in the REQUIRED order in all three cells — sync, driven and the owned twin print the iterator's deinit BEFORE \"after … loop\"; #5 REFUSED (\"cannot copy value of type `Counter` which implements NoCopy\"); #6 prints 4 / drop kept / caught — the base's count, the r1 leak gone; #7 prints 1 / done; #8 prints 2; #9 prints 7; #10 REFUSED naming the shared reference field and the rule. The lead's earlier probes (seven refusals, seven acceptance twins, the ledger spot-check) are unchanged from c71.\n\nMechanisms (agent addendum c78): the borrowing rules are judged by PATH (every returning path's leaf; one alias-resolving name resolver for both boundaries; one shared-reference-field hop walk asked by every write/receiver/`&var` entry, with the READ side's ICE fixed alongside); the window's overlap and identity questions go to `types._paths_overlap` and the record's `root_id`; the driven `for`'s seed + loop are one `ScopedBlock` so the resource is released on every exit route BEFORE the root charge ends (K137 now prints inside the walk), and the head is a transfer through `_check_value_transfer`; the try landing SPLITS the statement at each nested propagating `try` in evaluation order so a hoisted `try` owes only the clears of the moves in its own subject — every ordering codex named is reachable and pinned with drop counts (K152). Found by the sweep and filed, pre-existing: SL-348 (a SYNC abandoned call's already-moved argument is released by nothing). Gates on the tip: suite 2628 / 10 xfail / 0 fail, freestanding 36/36, battery 32/32. Awaiting the server gate + codex on r2.\n","created":"1790090860","id":"c80"},{"author":"agent:codex","body":"Requested changes on patch SL-318.p7 (revision 2)\n\nRequest changes — SL-318.p7 revision 2 (base 82a6ab86, applied 76b5f9b5).\n\nAll 16 original r1 reproductions/controls rerun successfully against the intended corrected behavior: the ten reported ordinary shapes are fixed. The following adjacent paths remain incorrect. Two independent Sol source reviews supplied candidates; I compiled/exercised the candidates below myself. Four non-borrow-syntax regressions were also run against exact base 82a6ab86.\n\n1. [P1] Copy-tier iterator heads are still bitwise loads, so the first loop frees the binding’s storage\n\nsawc/codegen/loops.py:168-197; sawc/typechecker/statements.py:4021-4036\n\nThe new checkpoint records a Copy transfer, but the sync loop still loads the head with _generate_expression and stores it without consuming the copy/retain decision before claiming cleanup ownership. Changing the new acceptance fixture’s static \"abc\" to interpolated \"abc {5}\" makes the full fixture segfault (exit -11). The reduced program below prints corrupted \"43 43\" on r2; exact base 82a6ab86 prints \"30 abc 5 0\". The static literal in the submitted fixture masks the freed heap storage. Consume the ordinary transfer decision at the actual iterator acquisition, not just in the checker.\n\n```saw\nstruct Words { text: String, index: Int }\nextension Words: Iterator {\n    type Item = Int\n    func next(&var self) -> Int? {\n        if self.index < self.text.len() {\n            self.index = self.index + 1\n            self.index\n        } else { None }\n    }\n}\nfunc walk(n: Int) sync {\n    let it = Words(text: \"abc {n}\", index: 0)\n    var total = 0\n    for x in it { total = total + x }\n    for x in it { total = total + x }\n    print(\"{total} {it.text} {it.index}\")\n}\nfunc main() { walk(5) }\n```\n\n2. [P1] Value-position for still bypasses the new NoCopy head checkpoint\n\nsawc/typechecker/statements.py:4084-4101; sawc/codegen/loops.py:350-383\n\nOnly _check_for_loop received the transfer checkpoint; _check_for_loop_as_expression did not. A named NoCopy iterator in `let got = for n in it { break n }` compiles and is destroyed twice. R2 output: \"drop counter / 7 / after loop / drop counter\"; exact base outputs \"7 / after loop / drop counter\". This is a wholly sync loop, not deferred SL-346’s spanning value-position case. Both statement and value forms acquire an owner and must enforce the same move/copy contract.\n\n```saw\nstruct Counter { index: Int }\nextension Counter: NoCopy {\n    func deinit(&var self) { print(\"drop counter\") }\n}\nextension Counter: Iterator {\n    type Item = Int\n    func next(&var self) -> Int? {\n        if self.index == 0 {\n            self.index = 1\n            7\n        } else { None }\n    }\n}\nfunc walk() sync {\n    let it = Counter(index: 0)\n    let got = for n in it { break n }\n    print(got ?? 0)\n    print(\"after loop\")\n}\nfunc main() { walk() }\n```\n\n3. [P1] A failing try behind ?? still forgets a later move that never ran\n\nsawc/coro_transform.py:2302-2312,2413-2441,7640-7647\n\n_ptry_stmt and _ptry skip conditional values. On the None path of `maybe ?? sink(try fail(), move r)`, fail() runs before move r, yet the whole-statement error landing still clears r. R2 prints \"4 / caught\", missing \"drop kept\"; exact base prints \"4 / drop kept / caught\". Decompose error sites within their guarded paths, without hoisting them outside their guards. This is a driven-body regression and is not SL-348’s pre-existing SYNC abandoned-argument issue.\n\n```saw\nimport std.task.{yield_now}\n\nstruct Res { name: String }\nextension Res: NoCopy {\n    func deinit(&var self) { print(\"drop {}\", self.name) }\n}\n\nstruct Bad { n: Int }\nextension Bad: Error {\n    func format(&self, into: &var StringBuilder) { try! into.append(\"bad\") }\n}\n\nfunc note(r: &Res) -> Int { r.name.len() }\nfunc fail() sync -> Result<Int, Bad> { Bad(n: 1) }\nfunc sink(n: Int, r: Res) sync -> Int { n }\n\nfunc work() -> Result<Int, Bad> {\n    var r = Res(name: \"kept\")\n    print(note(&r))\n    yield_now()\n    let maybe: Int? = None\n    let n = maybe ?? sink(try fail(), move r)\n    n\n}\n\nfunc main() {\n    match __saw_drive(work()) {\n        case Ok(n) -> print(n),\n        case Err(_) -> print(\"caught\")\n    }\n}\n```\n\n4. [P2] A propagating try inside a reference argument’s index produces invalid LLVM\n\nsawc/coro_transform.py:2452-2476,2562-2590,2730-2816\n\n`sink(&values[try fail_index()], move r)` fails compilation on r2 with LLVM’s `use of undefined value %caught_error` while exact base compiles and prints \"4 / drop kept / caught\". The new decomposition sees that the argument contains a try but its unconditional-child mapper has no ReferenceExpr descent; its place-index special handling applies only to a preceding reference sibling without a try. A borrowed place is not a value to hoist, but its evaluated indices still need position-preserving error decomposition. This is an observed compiler error, NOT a demonstrated runtime leak for this spelling.\n\n```saw\nimport std.task.{yield_now}\n\nstruct Res { name: String }\nextension Res: NoCopy {\n    func deinit(&var self) { print(\"drop {}\", self.name) }\n}\n\nstruct Bad { n: Int }\nextension Bad: Error {\n    func format(&self, into: &var StringBuilder) { try! into.append(\"bad\") }\n}\n\nfunc note(r: &Res) -> Int { r.name.len() }\nfunc fail_index() sync -> Result<Int, Bad> { Bad(n: 1) }\nfunc sink(value: &Int, r: Res) sync -> Int { 0 }\n\nfunc work() -> Result<Int, Bad> {\n    var r = Res(name: \"kept\")\n    print(note(&r))\n    let values: Vector<Int> = [9]\n    yield_now()\n    let n = sink(&values[try fail_index()], move r)\n    n\n}\n\nfunc main() {\n    match __saw_drive(work()) {\n        case Ok(n) -> print(n),\n        case Err(_) -> print(\"caught\")\n    }\n}\n```\n\n5. [P2] An alias-returning producer is certified without checking any returning path\n\nsawc/typechecker/borrowing.py:657-674\n\nThe producer check recognizes Alias as borrowing, but _producer_paths_missing_origin asks get_struct_info(ret.struct_name) before resolving the alias and returns success on None. The complete declaration below compiles even though bad returns only other.iter(), never a construction with lends self. Resolve the return type before proving its fields and paths; absence of struct information cannot mean origin proof succeeded. This finding is the missing declaration/origin check: attempts to drive this particular distinct alias are separately refused for lacking Iterator, so I am NOT claiming a demonstrated wrong-root runtime iteration.\n\n```saw\nstruct Owner { value: Int }\nborrows struct It { source: &Owner, done: Bool }\ntype Alias = It\n\nextension Owner {\n    func iter(&self) borrows -> It {\n        It(source: lends self, done: false)\n    }\n    func bad(&self, other: &Owner) borrows -> Alias {\n        Alias(other.iter())\n    }\n}\n\nfunc main() {}\n```\n\n6. [P2] The borrowing-type fence misses aliases to composites and deeper compositions\n\nsawc/typechecker/borrowing.py:191-250\n\nThe new resolver follows aliases only when asking whether the whole type is a borrowing struct. _first_borrowing_struct_in then descends the original alias rather than its target. `Hidden = Vector<It>` therefore passes as a stored field and function parameter; the complete NoCopy Holder program below compiles. The same fence also accepts a Holder field with nine nested Vector wrappers because depth > 8 returns safe. Both programs were compiled successfully after giving Holder its independently required NoCopy policy. Resolve aliases before descending composition, and do not treat exhaustion of a traversal bound as evidence that a type contains no borrow.\n\n```saw\nstruct Owner { value: Int }\nborrows struct It { source: &Owner, done: Bool }\ntype Hidden = Vector<It>\n\nstruct Holder {\n    value: Hidden\n}\n\nfunc accept(value: Hidden) -> Int { 0 }\nextension Holder: NoCopy {}\nfunc main() {}\n```\n\nThe independently verified depth-bound variant:\n\n```saw\nstruct Owner { value: Int }\nborrows struct It { source: &Owner, done: Bool }\n\nstruct Holder {\n    value: Vector<Vector<Vector<Vector<Vector<Vector<Vector<Vector<Vector<It>>>>>>>>>\n}\n\nextension Holder: NoCopy {}\nfunc main() {}\n```\n\n7. [P2] The producer exemption still admits stored borrowing values and mutation of their origin\n\nsawc/typechecker/borrowing.py:281-294; sawc/typechecker/statements.py:2049-2063\n\nThe exemption applies to every bare borrowing-typed expression in a same-result producer, not only the construction/return being proved. The new binding hook only catches self. Below, bad stores other.iter() in held, mutates other while held remains live, reads 99 through held, and returns a valid self-origin construction. R2 compiles and prints \"99 / 1\". Reject the local binding: the enclosing method’s return type cannot exempt unrelated borrow values from the direct-head/window rule.\n\n```saw\nstruct Owner { value: Int }\nborrows struct It { source: &Owner, done: Bool }\n\nextension Owner {\n    func iter(&self) borrows -> It {\n        It(source: lends self, done: false)\n    }\n    func bad(&self, other: &var Owner) borrows -> It {\n        var held = other.iter()\n        other.value = 99\n        print(held.next()!)\n        It(source: lends self, done: false)\n    }\n}\n\nextension It: Iterator {\n    type Item = Int\n    func next(&var self) -> Int? {\n        if self.done { None } else {\n            self.done = true\n            self.source.value\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nfunc main() {\n    let root = Owner(value: 1)\n    var other = Owner(value: 2)\n    for x in root.bad(&var other) { print(x) }\n}\n```\n\n8. [P2] A local shadow of a module-static window root still falsely conflicts\n\nsawc/typechecker/borrowing.py:901-906,1022-1027; sawc/windows.py:341-345\n\nBoth root identity lookups consult only current_scope. The static ROOT therefore opens a window with no binding identity; an independent local ROOT constructed from its value is still compared by name. R2 rejects the local field assignment below as a write to the borrowed static. Renaming only the local binding and its uses to local compiles and prints 3; the window and static are unchanged. Resolve statics and locals to distinct stable identities before comparing projections.\n\n```saw\nstruct Owner { value: Int }\nborrows struct It { source: &Owner, done: Bool }\n\nextension Owner {\n    func iter(&self) borrows -> It {\n        It(source: lends self, done: false)\n    }\n}\n\nextension It: Iterator {\n    type Item = Int\n    func next(&var self) -> Int? {\n        if self.done { None } else {\n            self.done = true\n            self.source.value\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nstatic ROOT: Owner = Owner(value: 1)\n\nfunc main() {\n    for n in ROOT.iter() {\n        var ROOT = Owner(value: ROOT.value + 1)\n        ROOT.value = ROOT.value + n\n        print(ROOT.value)\n    }\n}\n```\n\n9. [P2] The shared-field walk mistakes an unsafe pointer’s pointee for owner storage\n\nsawc/typechecker/borrowing.py:773-815; sawc/typechecker/statements.py:2223-2290\n\nThe new walk returns immediately on self.source, before checking whether a later pointer indirection leaves that referent’s storage. It rejects the unsafe pointee assignment below as a write through shared Owner. The ordinary `func poke(owner: &Owner) unsafe { owner.p[0] = 7 }` control compiles on r2; the existing immutable-lvalue walk explicitly stops at UnsafePointer indexing. Preserve that storage boundary: reading a pointer field does not make its independently pointed-to storage part of the shared Owner.\n\n```saw\nstruct Owner { p: UnsafePointer<Int> }\nborrows struct It { source: &Owner, done: Bool }\n\nextension It {\n    func poke(&var self) unsafe {\n        self.source.p[0] = 7\n    }\n}\n\nfunc main() {}\n```\n\n10. [P2] Alias-spelled shared reference fields are falsely reported as having no lent place\n\nsawc/typechecker/types.py:956-994; sawc/typechecker/borrowing.py:657-663,801-807,840-848\n\n_borrowing_field_referent explicitly permits top-level aliases resolving to shared &T, but check_borrowing_struct_decl inspects the raw field kind. The legal declaration below is rejected with \"holds no lent place\". The producer proof, permission walk, and new codegen reference-field check likewise inspect raw field kinds, so accepting it only at the declaration would leave those consumers inconsistent. Use the resolved field category consistently. This is a false rejection, NOT a demonstrated permission bypass.\n\n```saw\nstruct Owner { value: Int }\ntype OwnerRef = &Owner\n\nborrows struct It {\n    source: OwnerRef\n    done: Bool\n}\n\nfunc main() {}\n```\n\nVerification:\n- Exact patch applied in isolated .build/review-sl318p7-r2; no implementation edits or merge.\n- 16 r1 repros/controls rerun; intended corrected behavior observed in all.\n- Focused revised fixture suite: 58 passed, 1 known xfail (K141 / SL-340), 59 total.\n- tools/test_coro_shapes.py, tools/test_transfer_decisions.py, tools/test_producer_taxonomy.py, tools/test_window_seam.py: all exit 0.\n- Concrete new programs above compiled/executed; compile-only declaration checks are identified as such. Exact-base comparisons confirm the Copy corruption, value-for double destruction, conditional-try missing destruction, and reference-index LLVM failure are introduced by the patch.\n- Tracker currently says r2 tests pending, with tested_commit still 2781b4ce. Its attached full-suite output is therefore r1 evidence, not r2 validation.\n- Existing SL-340/341/345/346/348 remain separately scoped. No claim that the U3 patch completes epic SL-318.\n\nArtifacts: .build/sl318p7-r2-probes.json; .build/sl318p7-r2-suite.log; .build/sl318p7-r2-test_*.py.log; .build/review-sl318p7-r2/.build/scratch/review_*.saw. All examples in this review are standalone and use ordinary public source syntax. No implementation changes, commit, push, or merge performed.\n","created":"1790093090","id":"c81"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"LEAD ACK of codex r2 on SL-318.p7 (Sep 22): all ten accepted as filed; every one is an adjacent path of an r2 family and reads as three mechanisms. (F1) The head transfer is DECIDED but not CONSUMED: `_check_value_transfer` records the Copy/NoCopy answer for a `for` head, and codegen's iterator acquisition still loads the head bitwise before claiming cleanup ownership (#1 — a Copy-tier binding is freed by its first loop; codex's interpolated-String twin of the fixture segfaults), while the value-position lowering never asked the checkpoint at all (#2 — a NoCopy head in `let got = for n in it { break n }` is destroyed twice; a wholly sync loop, not SL-346's). The decision must be consumed at the acquisition, in the statement form, the value form and the driven twin. (F2) Positional relinquish stops at GUARDS and at PLACES: the leaf split skips conditional values, so `maybe ?? sink(try fail(), move r)`'s None path clears a move that never ran (#3), and it has no descent into a `ReferenceExpr`'s index, so `sink(&values[try fail_index()], move r)` emits `use of undefined value %caught_error` (#4) — the decomposition must descend into every guarded path WITHOUT hoisting an error site outside its guard, and into an index expression without hoisting the borrowed place. (F3) The borrowing rules resolve aliases LATE or NOT AT ALL, and two exemptions reach past their subject: the producer origin check asks `get_struct_info` on the alias name and treats \"no info\" as proof (#5); `_first_borrowing_struct_in` descends the alias rather than its target and a depth-bound exhaustion answers \"safe\" (#6 — `Hidden = Vector<It>` and nine nested `Vector`s both pass the fence); the producer exemption admits every bare borrowing-typed expression in the body rather than the value being PROVED, so `var held = other.iter()` inside a producer stores a borrow and mutates its origin (#7); the window root's identity lookups consult `current_scope` only, so a module static has no identity and a local shadow of its name conflicts by text (#8); the shared-field walk returns at the reference hop before an `UnsafePointer` indexing leaves the referent's storage, unlike `_immutable_lvalue_root` (#9); and an alias-spelled `&T` field is judged by its raw kind at the declaration while `_borrowing_field_referent` resolves it, so a legal field is refused and the consumers disagree (#10 — use ONE resolved field category everywhere).\n\nCodex's programs are staged in the worktree's `.build/scratch/lead/x_codex_r2_f<N>.saw` (#6 has an `a`/`b` pair). A FRESH lead-model agent (`agent:claude-sl318u3d`) resumes in `sl318u3` with the three mechanisms as its commit plan — each fixed at the mechanism with the adjacent-position sweep that r2 owed and did not do — per-commit gate + terminal battery, addendum here; the lead re-runs all twenty programs and revises to r3.\n","created":"1790096974","id":"c82"},{"author":"agent:sawtracker-tests","body":"Tests passed for SL-318.p7 (revision 2)","created":"1790097785","id":"c83"},{"author":"agent:claude-sl318u3d","body":"U3 addendum — codex r2 on SL-318.p7 (agent:claude-sl318u3d, Sep 22). Branch `sl318u3`, tip **4eb8094c** on top of the reviewed 6472c14c; three mechanisms, one commit each, every commit gated (full suite + freestanding both arches), terminal battery on the tip: **32 stages GREEN in 3426s**. Columns below are (base / r2 / now).\n\n**F1 — the head transfer is CONSUMED at the acquisition (#1, #2) — 05ef5043.** One acquisition routine (`_acquire_head_iterator`) is shared by the statement and the value lowering and consumes entry 20's stamp before the loop claims cleanup ownership; `_check_for_loop_as_expression` asks the checkpoint as `_check_for_loop` does. Not revised: p7 stays at r2 for the lead's re-validation and revise to r3.\n","created":"1790105587","id":"c84"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"Revised patch SL-318.p7 to revision 3","created":"1790105787","id":"c85"}],"created":"1789738775","description":"USER RULING (Sep 18): revisit the coroutine transform's discovery seams as an EPIC rather than continue per-site funnel fixes. Four units, all ruled as the lead recommended: (1) ONE DISCOVERY LEDGER — a single pass computes the closed frame table (key, declaration, really-suspends, buildable, reason) before any body is lowered; classifier, closure walk, generic promotion, rejectors and drive-site rewriting READ it and derive nothing; a battery lane fails on any transform site that reads the raw suspension bit or a symbol name directly (the producer-taxonomy / astgraft precedent). (2) TOTALITY — every statement and expression shape is either splittable or refused by ONE rejector reading ONE shape table; a callee the walk cannot frame is a compile error, never a plain call whose park blocks the executor (closes SL-287, SL-316; the K33-K37 XFAIL family are rows). (3) COVERAGE UNITS INSIDE THE EPIC — SL-215 (inline try/catch split) and SL-317 (iterator state in the frame for a collection for) are epic units, NOT standalone point fixes; sawtracker's build (currently stopped at those two) is the epic's acceptance test and WAITS for it — the user ruled a point fix is a waste of time. (4) A THREE-VALUED ANSWER AT THE EFFECT SOURCE — the graph answers once (really suspends / only through a closure call / does not) instead of one bit meaning might plus design 206's second question bolted beside it. Census (Sep 18): coro_transform.py 12,224 lines, a 212-method _FrameBuilder, a 1,032-line transform_program with nine nested closures, ~20 distinct suspension/frame-key predicates, 7 split shapes (if, if-let, guard-let, while, RANGE for, match, BLOCK try-catch), 21 scattered refusals, 17 governing designs (44..224). The lowering is stable; every finding this week (SL-280, SL-274 keying sites, SL-306 + its review, SL-215, SL-317, SL-287, SL-316) was a discovery seam. Brief: designs/275-coro-discovery-uniformity.md (lead, next).
